EditorsÆ Foreword
The 12th  issue of  ôEthnomethodology ö results of the further
work with the themes of cultural heritage and culture-inheriting
activity, of discussing both utmost horizons of possible development of
this socially important activity and its specific structure and functioning.
One of the new dimensions of this theme consists in the widening of the
general framework of discussion of inheriting activity through including
the problems of globalization. In some articles of the issue an idea is defended
that it is impossible to understand categorical and pragmatic meaning of
the very concept of heritage without making clear the connection between
globalization processes and the development of the inheriting activity
institutes.
The issue opens with the summary of a discussion of the problems of
cultural heritage on the Kazan Forum
(2003) presented by E.L.Ivanova.
In the article by A.A.Piskoppel,
Globalization as reality and cultural heritage, 
relations between
globalization processes and the inheriting activity with its integrative
function of putting together and ordering different forms of this kind
of culturopractics and the problems of investigating these relations are
discussed. The inheriting activity is considered as securing continuity

φε±≥ⁿ Γ√∩εδφ σ≥ ⌠≤φΩ÷Φ■ Ωεφ±≥≡≤Ω≥ΦΓφε being permanent transformation of
social evolution into social development 
through the
mechanism of cultural heritage.
ôContinuityö with respect to the process of globalization is under-
stood here as a conscious preservation  in new circumstances of
regional specifics within that very world from which the globalization
trends do arise. Thereby the unitary process of globalization-regiona-
lization comes forth wherein each of the partial processes is a
necessary side complementary to another one.
The article by A.A.Piskoppel,
V.R.Rokitiansky, 
L.P.Shedrovitsky , Inheriting activity and
its fundamentals, presents a discussion of philosophic and
theoretico-methodological problems arising in thinking over
of utmost horizons of inheriting activity and of possible approaches
to development of the concept of heritage. In the context of the
opposition of culturology and culturotechnics the meaning of
inheriting activity 
as a
culturotechnical activity is considered which can be a
mechanism of social activity and of its development on different levels,
of different generalization and different scale. Special attention is
paid  to the question of continuity and to a hypothesis that it is the
concept of continuity whereby the value specific to culture inheriting
activity is expressed. Thus

activity is expressed. Thus considered the inheriting activity itself performs a
function of a constructive mechanism of making for continuity relation
between past and future in the life way of a society.
In the article Inheriting primals by
V.R.Rokitiansky an attempt is made to trace micromechanisms securing
continuity in the history of human thinking, its unity in change. As a
possible candidate to the role of those constants which new generations of
thinkers inherit from their predecessors even in case of conscious rejection of
their conceptions, notions and arguments so called ôlogismoiö
(ôargumentsö or ôcalculationsö in
Greek) are considered as a kind of thinking habits inherent to a language
or intellectual culture. The theme is discussed using texts by pre-Socratic
philosophers wherein they attempted to express their intuition of a principle
(archτ) 
as something 
primal, fundamental and determining.
In S.V.Sokolovskiy’s article The Legal
Construction of Aboriginality in
Social Sciences the concept of aboriginality in Russian politics and law
within the framework of academic debates on the issue and administrative
practices is discussed. Academic, legal and political representations of
minorities, striving to attain the status of indigenous are explored. Russian
legal system has several legal categories based on ethnicity, whose members
claim special rights. In order to ensure

these rights, the state administration needs to periodically collect reliable information
on the numbers of people in such categories using population. So, a case study of the
development of new lists of ethnic categories and languages in the Russian
population census of 2002 is included as an example of using of academic knowledge
in construction of social classifications that underpin such phenomena as ethnicity and
nationalism in contemporary Russia.
The issue is concluded by a document published by V.Rokitiansky, School
diary of my father. It is a diary of sixteen years old schoolboy written in 1934-35. It
contains impressions from surrounding life, a story of  relations with schoolmates,
thoughts about future. The goal of the publication is to investigate the possibili-
ties inherent to one more plane of  cultureinheriting  activity which is related not to
matters being traditional to it, such as material culture or social institutions but
to the history and continuity of “human soul”, of types of personality, conduct and
feeling.
